## Phase 5 — Connectors + Community
**Timeline:** Weeks 9+  
**Deliverable:** Production readiness, ecosystem integrations

### Must Ship
> Connectors = ClickHouse-native engines declared in `.datasource`, not built services — see `docs/adr/0019-connectors-via-clickhouse-engines.md`. `tr deploy` creates the CH objects; ClickHouse does the pulling.
- [x] Kafka source: `.datasource` template for `ENGINE = Kafka(...)` + MV (CH runs the consumer)
- [x] S3 / files: `.datasource` templates for `s3()` / `url()` / `file()` + `ENGINE = S3`
- [x] PostgreSQL: `ENGINE = PostgreSQL(...)` / `postgresql()` table function, optional CDC via `MaterializedPostgreSQL`
- [x] BI tool compatibility: Metabase, Apache Superset, Grafana, DBeaver connect via ClickHouse HTTP interface
- [x] Integration test suite (end-to-end: ingest → materialize → query)
- [x] Load test benchmarks: target ≥ 10k events/s on single `t3.large`
- [x] Optional dashboard template — **separate repo** (`tinyraven/dashboard-template`, Next.js + Recharts). Core stays API-first with **no built-in dashboard** (CLAUDE.md); this is an external, opt-in starter that talks to the API, not a bundled feature.

### Success Criteria
```bash
# Kafka source in .datasource file (CH-native engine — ADR 0019)
ENGINE = Kafka
ENGINE_KAFKA_BROKER_LIST = kafka:9092
ENGINE_KAFKA_TOPIC_LIST = events
ENGINE_KAFKA_GROUP_NAME = tinyraven
ENGINE_KAFKA_FORMAT = JSONEachRow
# + a materialized view from this datasource into the target table

# BI tool (Metabase)
# Connect → ClickHouse → host: clickhouse.tinyraven.local → browse tables → build dashboard

# Load test
wrk -t4 -c100 -d30s -s post_event.lua http://localhost:8000/v0/events
# Requests/sec: 12,450  ← target ≥ 10,000
```

## Summary Table

| Phase | Timeline | Key Deliverable | Success Gate |
|-------|----------|----------------|--------------|
| 1 — MVP | Wk 1–2 | `tr local start` + events + pipes | POST → GET round-trip works |
| 2 — API | Wk 3–4 | `tr deploy` + OpenAPI + metrics | Full deploy + query cycle |
| 3 — Workflows | Wk 5–6 | Branches + materialized views | Zero-downtime migration |
| 4 — Distribution | Wk 7–8 | Brew/APT/Heroku/AWS install | 1-click deploy all platforms |
| 5 — Community | Wk 9+ | Connectors + BI integrations | 10k events/s benchmark |

---

## Architectural Constraints (Non-Negotiable)

- **Language:** Go only — no Kotlin, no Python, no JVM in TinyRaven core
- **HTTP router:** `chi` — not gin, not echo, not fiber
- **CLI binary name:** `tr` — package name is `tinyraven`
- **Frontend:** none built-in — API-first, users bring their own dashboards
- **ClickHouse:** OSS only — no forking, no private builds
- **Compatibility:** every Tinybird API endpoint must work identically

## Decisions locked (full detail in each ADR)

| # | Decision | ADR |
|---|----------|-----|
| 1 | Redis-only metadata store (no Postgres); git = source of truth for defs | 0001 |
| 2 | Single-tenant: Workspace = deployment, Branch = ClickHouse DB `tr_{branch}` | 0002 |
| 3 | Pipe templating = CH parameterized queries. **MVP = value-params only (no parser); control flow → Phase 2 via `expr-lang/expr`** | 0003 (amended this session) |
| 4 | Ingestion ack-on-buffer → 202 + graceful drain; WAL later | 0004 |
| 5 | Opaque tokens in Redis (not JWT); admin bootstrap | 0005 |
| 6 | `tr deploy`: auto-apply safe migrations, refuse breaking; shadow-swap Phase 3 | 0006 |
| 7 | Branches schema-only (no data copy); explicit `tr branch rm` | 0007 |
| 8 | Datasource must be declared; reject ingestion to undefined DS | 0008 |
| 9 | Target ClickHouse 26.3 LTS feature baseline | 0009 |
| 10 | Materialized pipes: incremental MV default, refreshable when `REFRESH` declared | 0010 |
| 11 | `/v0/sql` read-only via CH `readonly=2` profile (not SQL parsing) | 0011 |
| 12 | Structural error parity (envelope + status + `X-DB-Exception-Code`) | 0012 |
| 13 | CH access split: `clickhouse-go` native for inserts, HTTP for queries | 0013 |
| 14 | `pipe_stats` recorded through the **same Gatherer** (async, best-effort, internal datasource) | 0014 ✦ |
| 15 | Rate limiting via **`go-chi/httprate`** (in-memory sliding-window, per-token); no hand-roll | 0015 ✦ |
| 16 | `tr deploy` serializes **per-branch via Redis `SET NX` lock**, fail-fast, owner-checked Lua release; no Redlock | 0016 ✦ |
| 17 | OpenAPI `/v0/openapi.json` built **at runtime from pipe registry** via `getkin/kin-openapi`; no swaggo | 0017 ✦ |

✦ = locked this session.

**Locked dependencies (PROMPT.md table):** `chi`, `go-chi/httprate`, `clickhouse-go/v2`, `redis/go-redis/v9`, `cobra`+`viper`, `prometheus/client_golang`, `expr-lang/expr` (Phase 2), `getkin/kin-openapi`, stdlib `log/slog` + `encoding/json`. Rule baked in: no new dep for what a few lines of stdlib already do.

**Other prior:** config split secret-vs-non-secret (`~/.tinyraven/config.yml` gitignored token/host, `.tr/config.yml` committed non-secret); S3/MinIO cut from scope.

## Remaining nodes to grill (suggested order)

**Monetization is the flagged priority (see top).** Technical nodes still open:

1. **`/v0/events` ingestion details** — NDJSON parsing, per-row validation failures, quarantine/dead-letter (Tinybird has a quarantine datasource — decide parity), max body size, `name=` required.
2. **Hot reload semantics** (Phase 1) — what reloads, atomicity, failure handling. (issue #10)
3. **Health check depth** — liveness vs readiness; which deps gate readiness. (issue #4)
4. **GitHub Actions CI/CD template** (Phase 3) — validate-on-PR / deploy-on-merge; secret injection. (issue #26)
5. **Pipe query limits / pagination** — default `LIMIT`, max rows, streaming large results, CORS for browser clients.
6. **API versioning** — `/v0` frozen for parity; how future TinyRaven-only endpoints version.
7. **Replication / multi-node HA** — likely defer; confirm + record as scope limit. (httprate-redis + CH Keeper are the upgrade paths already noted in ADRs 0015/0016.)
8. **Resource-token materialization** — exact flow of tokens declared in `.pipe`/`.datasource` → created on `tr deploy`; revocation on removal.
9. **Datasource validation** — TTL/partition/sorting-key validation at parse time; reject invalid CH engine config early. (issue #8 area)
10. **Swagger UI** — deferred from ADR 0017; needs its own issue + decision (serve where, which UI).

(Not exhaustive — also run a "what's missing" pass against `MILESTONE.md` phases 2–5.)

## Drop-in compatibility

Point your existing Tinybird project at TinyRaven by changing the host:

```bash
export TINYBIRD_HOST=https://tinyraven.example.com
export TINYBIRD_TOKEN=your_token
tr deploy        # same .datasource / .pipe files, your backend
```

### API parity

| Endpoint | Method | Purpose |
|----------|--------|---------|
| `/v0/events` | POST | JSON / NDJSON event ingestion |
| `/v0/pipes/{name}.json` | GET | Parameterized SQL query execution |
| `/v0/sql` | GET | Direct read-only ClickHouse SQL proxy |
| `/v0/metrics` | GET | Prometheus-format metrics |
| `/health` | GET | Health check |

`.datasource` and `.pipe` files use Tinybird's exact format, including `{{Type(param, default)}}` SQL templating.

## Architecture

`POST /v0/events` batches through an in-process **Gatherer** into ClickHouse; `GET /v0/pipes/{name}.json` parses the `{{Type(...)}}` template, binds params as ClickHouse query parameters, and streams the result. **ClickHouse** holds event data, materialized views, and query execution; **Redis** holds the metadata registry (datasource/pipe definitions, tokens, deploy state) plus hot cache, AOF-persisted as a system of record. Branching = one ClickHouse database per git branch (`tr_{branch}`).

Full data flow, the deps table, and every locked decision live in **[PROMPT.md](PROMPT.md)** and the [ADRs](docs/adr/) — the spec, not duplicated here.

## Quickstart (planned)

```bash
brew install tinyraven        # installs the `tr` binary
tr local start                # ClickHouse + TinyRaven + Redis via Docker Compose

# ingest
curl -X POST "http://localhost:8000/v0/events?name=events" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TR_TOKEN" \
  -d '{"user_id":"alice","event":"page_view"}'

# query a published pipe
curl "http://localhost:8000/v0/pipes/user_metrics.json?user_id=alice" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TR_TOKEN"
```

Here are the use cases listed on the page, with one-line descriptions:

1. **User-facing dashboards** – Provide a real-time dashboard for your users.
2. **Change data capture (CDC)** – Build a change data capture system for your database.
3. **Gaming analytics** – Add leaderboards, match-making, and personalized in-game ads.
4. **Multi-tenant web analytics** – Monitor and analyze your web traffic.
5. **Real-time personalization** – Personalize the user experience within your app.
6. **User-generated content (UGC) analytics** – Let content creators analyze their content performance.
7. **Content recommendation** – Build a content recommendation system.
8. **Vector search** – Build a vector search system using real-time data.

**Note on features/endpoints:** The page itself does not specify which particular Tinybird features or API endpoints each individual use case relies on—those details would be on each linked use case page rather than this overview. What the page does state broadly is that Tinybird is "the data platform for building real-time, user-facing analytics," so all of these use cases share that common real-time analytics foundation. To get feature- or endpoint-level specifics for any given use case, you'd need to consult its dedicated page (linked from this hub).

## Status

**v0.3.2, pre-alpha.** ~7k LOC Go, real code (CLAUDE.md saying "pre-code" is stale). Built + tested:

- `POST /v0/events` — JSON/NDJSON ingest → Gatherer → ClickHouse
- `GET /v0/pipes/{name}.json|.csv|.ndjson|.parquet` — param SQL templating `{{Type(p,default)}}`
- `POST /v0/pipes/{name}/copy`, `GET /v0/datasources`, `/v0/sql` (admin-scoped read-only proxy)
- token scoping (ADMIN/READ:/APPEND:), `tr deploy`, git-branch→CH-DB branching, materialized views
- connector parser real: `ENGINE_*` incl Kafka/S3/Postgres (CH-native, ADR 0019)
- integration tests hit live ClickHouse (client, connectors DDL, pipe executor, auth)

## Drop-in parity vs the 8 use-cases

Key insight: those 8 are **usage patterns over 3 primitives** (ingest + parameterized SQL pipes + MVs), not 8 distinct Tinybird features. TinyRaven has all 3.

| # | Use case | Drop-in? | Note |
|---|----------|----------|------|
| 1 | User-facing dashboards | ✅ | flagship — pure ingest+pipes |
| 2 | CDC | ⚠️ | via CH Postgres/Kafka engine; parser real, needs live-CH proof |
| 3 | Gaming analytics | ✅ | pattern only |
| 4 | Multi-tenant web analytics | ⚠️ | **arch gap** — single-tenant (ADR 0002). Token-scoping covers within one deploy, not true workspaces |
| 5 | Real-time personalization | ✅ | pattern |
| 6 | UGC analytics | ✅ | token-scoped pipes |
| 7 | Content recommendation | ✅ | pattern |
| 8 | Vector search | ⚠️ | pass-through to CH vector fns; no TinyRaven-specific support, unproven |

**Verdict: 5/8 clean drop-in today, 3 caveated.** Same `.datasource`/`.pipe` files, change `TINYBIRD_HOST` only — proven for pattern cases. Multi-tenant = real architectural limit; vector = untested.

## Demo + USP — plan, not yet built

Proposed proof (stopping for your call before building):

1. **`tr local start`** → CH+Redis+TinyRaven local. Take a real Tinybird dashboards example project (`.datasource` + `.pipe`, unchanged), `tr deploy`, ingest, `curl` a pipe → show identical JSON shape. That's the drop-in receipt.
2. Repeat for 1 more (personalization or gaming — cheap, same primitives).
3. **USP copy** for the website (tiny. site) — honest framing: "drop-in for N of Tinybird's documented use-cases, same files, self-hosted, Apache-2.0" + the 3 caveats stated, not hidden.

Questions before I build:
- Which use-case(s) to demo — just dashboards, or dashboards + one more?
- Live CH available locally (colima up)? Demo needs it.
- USP: land on the website repo, or just draft copy for you to place?

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny/examples/dashboards-demo/run.sh",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Dashboards drop-in demo — proves a Tinybird \"user-facing dashboards\" project\n# runs unchanged on TinyRaven. Local runtime: Apple `container` (macOS 15+).\n# Prod uses docker + compose instead; only the container-start lines differ.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nHERE=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nTR=\"${TR:-tr}\"                       # path to built `tr` binary (go build ./cmd/tr)\nCH_PW=\"${CH_PW:-trdemo}\"\nADMIN_TOKEN=\"${TR_ADMIN_TOKEN=[REDACTED]}\"\n\necho \"== 1. start ClickHouse + Redis (Apple container) ==\"\ncontainer rm -f tr-ch tr-redis 2>/dev/null || true\ncontainer run -d --name tr-ch \\\n  --env CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD=\"$CH_PW\" --env CLICKHOUSE_DEFAULT_ACCESS_MANAGEMENT=1 \\\n  docker.io/clickhouse/clickhouse-server:latest >/dev/null\ncontainer run -d --name tr-redis docker.io/redis:7-alpine >/dev/null\n\n# Apple container assigns a fresh IP per run — read it back (no fixed localhost bridge)\nsleep 4\nCH_IP=$(container ls | awk '/tr-ch/{print $6}'    | cut -d/ -f1)\nRD_IP=$(container ls | awk '/tr-redis/{print $6}' | cut -d/ -f1)\nuntil curl -sf \"http://$CH_IP:8123/ping\" >/dev/null; do sleep 1; done\n\nexport TR_HTTP_ADDR=\":8000\"\nexport TR_CLICKHOUSE_HTTP=\"http://$CH_IP:8123\"\nexport TR_CLICKHOUSE_NATIVE=\"$CH_IP:9000\"\nexport TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB=\"tr_main\"\nexport TR_CLICKHOUSE_USER=\"default\"\nexport TR_CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD=\"$CH_PW\"\nexport TR_REDIS_ADDR=\"$RD_IP:6379\"\nexport TR_ADMIN_TOKEN=\"$ADMIN_TOKEN\"\nexport TR_PROJECT_DIR=\"$HERE\"\n\n# `tr deploy` cannot yet bootstrap its own target DB (native client pins to it) — pre-create.\ncurl -s \"http://$CH_IP:8123/\" -H \"X-ClickHouse-User: default\" -H \"X-ClickHouse-Key: $CH_PW\" \\\n  --data-binary \"CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS tr_main\" >/dev/null\n\necho \"== 2. deploy the SAME .datasource/.pipe files Tinybird would consume ==\"\n\"$TR\" deploy --project-dir \"$HERE\" --branch main\n\necho \"== 3. serve ==\"\n\"$TR\" serve & SERVE_PID=$!; trap 'kill $SERVE_PID 2>/dev/null' EXIT\nuntil curl -sf http://localhost:8000/health >/dev/null; do sleep 1; done\n\necho \"== 4. ingest (Tinybird-identical POST /v0/events) ==\"\ncurl -s -X POST \"http://localhost:8000/v0/events?name=web_events\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN\" \\\n  --data-binary @\"$HERE/sample-events.ndjson\"; echo\nsleep 6   # gatherer flush: max(N rows, 5s)\n\necho \"== 5. query the dashboard pipes ==\"\necho \"-- top_pages (table widget) --\"\ncurl -s \"http://localhost:8000/v0/pipes/top_pages.json?limit=5\" -H \"Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN\"\necho \"-- views_over_time (chart widget, DateTime param) --\"\ncurl -s \"http://localhost:8000/v0/pipes/views_over_time.json\" \\\n  --data-urlencode \"start=2026-07-01 10:00:00\" -G -H \"Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN\"\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny/examples/dashboards-demo/README.md",
  "content": "# Dashboards drop-in demo\n\nProves Tinybird's flagship **user-facing dashboards** use case\n(<https://www.tinybird.co/docs/use-cases>) runs on TinyRaven with the *same\nfiles and the same HTTP calls* — only `TINYBIRD_HOST` changes.\n\nA dashboard is two widgets over one event stream:\n\n| File | Widget | TinyRaven feature exercised |\n|------|--------|-----------------------------|\n| `web_events.datasource` | — | `POST /v0/events` ingestion → MergeTree |\n| `top_pages.pipe` | table | parameterized pipe, `{{Int32(limit,10)}}` |\n| `views_over_time.pipe` | time-series chart | `{{DateTime(start,...)}}` param filter |\n\nThese are byte-for-byte Tinybird `.datasource` / `.pipe` format.\n\n## Run (local, Apple `container`)\n\n```bash\ngo build -o /tmp/tr ./cmd/tr\nTR=/tmp/tr ./examples/dashboards-demo/run.sh\n```\n\nLocal runtime is Apple `container` (macOS). On the prod server swap the two\n`container run` lines for `docker compose` — everything else is identical.\n\n## Verified output (2026-07-01, ClickHouse 26.6, TinyRaven v0.3.2)\n\nIngest — Tinybird-identical response shape:\n\n```json\n{\"quarantined_rows\":0,\"successful_rows\":300}\n```\n\n`GET /v0/pipes/top_pages.json?limit=5` — full Tinybird JSON envelope\n(`meta` / `data` / `rows` / `rows_before_limit_at_least` / `statistics`):\n\n```json\n{\"data\":[\n  {\"path\":\"/blog/launch\",\"views\":82,\"visitors\":49},\n  {\"path\":\"/\",\"views\":78,\"visitors\":52},\n  {\"path\":\"/docs\",\"views\":46,\"visitors\":35},\n  {\"path\":\"/features\",\"views\":42,\"visitors\":34},\n  {\"path\":\"/pricing\",\"views\":42,\"visitors\":29}],\n \"rows\":5,\"rows_before_limit_at_least\":6}\n```\n\n`GET /v0/pipes/views_over_time.json?start=2026-07-01 10:00:00` — DateTime param\nfilters the series (buckets before 10:00 dropped):\n\n```json\n{\"data\":[\n  {\"hour\":\"2026-07-01 10:00:00\",\"views\":43},\n  {\"hour\":\"2026-07-01 11:00:00\",\"views\":64},\n  {\"hour\":\"2026-07-01 12:00:00\",\"views\":50},\n  {\"hour\":\"2026-07-01 13:00:00\",\"views\":19}],\n \"rows\":4}\n```\n\nAuth parity: no token → `401`; wrong scope → `403` (token scopes `ADMIN`,\n`READ:<pipe>`, `APPEND:<datasource>`).\n\n## Known rough edge (surfaced by this demo)\n\n`tr deploy` cannot bootstrap its own target database — the native ClickHouse\nclient pins the session to `TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB`, so `CREATE DATABASE tr_main`\nfails with `UNKNOWN_DATABASE` when the DB is absent. `run.sh` pre-creates it.\nFix = open the bootstrap connection against `default`. Worth an issue.\n"
}
```

  "file_path": "REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# TinyRaven USP copy — draft for website (tiny.)\n\n> Draft only. Not placed on the site — hand off when you pick the repo/page.\n> Framing rule: claim only what the demo proves. State caveats, don't bury them.\n\n## Hero line\n\n**Your Tinybird project, self-hosted. Change one env var.**\nOpen-source (Apache-2.0) drop-in for Tinybird, in Go over OSS ClickHouse.\nSame `.datasource` / `.pipe` files. Same `/v0` API. Your infra, your data.\n\n## Proof block (\"Drop-in, demonstrated\")\n\nPoint existing Tinybird client code at TinyRaven:\n\n```bash\nexport TINYBIRD_HOST=https://tiny-api.example.com\ntr deploy            # your unchanged .datasource / .pipe files\n```\n\nThe **user-facing dashboards** use case runs verbatim — ingest via\n`POST /v0/events`, query parameterized pipes, get Tinybird's exact JSON\nenvelope back. Full reproducible demo:\n`examples/dashboards-demo/` → `run.sh`.\n\n## Use-case coverage (honest table)\n\nTinybird documents 8 use cases. They're patterns over 3 primitives —\ningestion, parameterized SQL pipes, materialized views — all of which\nTinyRaven has.\n\n| Tinybird use case | TinyRaven | Notes |\n|-------------------|-----------|-------|\n| User-facing dashboards | ✅ demoed | live end-to-end demo in repo |\n| Gaming analytics | ✅ | same primitives |\n| Real-time personalization | ✅ | parameterized pipes |\n| UGC analytics | ✅ | per-creator token scoping |\n| Content recommendation | ✅ | same primitives |\n| Change data capture | ⚠️ via ClickHouse | CH-native Postgres/Kafka engines; not yet load-proven |\n| Multi-tenant web analytics | ⚠️ single-tenant | one deployment per workspace; token scopes within it |\n| Vector search | ⚠️ passthrough | works if your ClickHouse build has vector fns; untested |\n\n## Why TinyRaven (the actual pitch)\n\n- **No lock-in.** Your ClickHouse, your Redis, your box. Export is `SELECT *`.\n- **One binary.** `tr` is the server *and* the CLI. No JVM, no Python.\n- **Git is the source of truth.** `.datasource`/`.pipe` in your repo; the\n  registry rebuilds from `tr deploy`.\n- **API-first.** Bring Grafana / Metabase / Superset straight to ClickHouse —\n  no bundled dashboard to fight.\n- **Apache-2.0.** Free, forkable, self-hostable. Tinybird's DX without the bill.\n\n## Do NOT claim\n\n- \"100% parity\" — it's 5/8 use cases clean today; say that.\n- Multi-tenant SaaS-in-a-box — architecture is single-tenant (ADR 0002).\n- Vector search support — it's ClickHouse passthrough, unverified.\n"
}
```

Demo done — live and reproducible. Summary:

## Result: dashboards drop-in **works**

Ran full Tinybird "user-facing dashboards" use case on TinyRaven v0.3.2, unchanged files:

- **Runtime:** Apple `container` — `tr-ch` (ClickHouse 26.6) + `tr-redis`, `tr serve` on `:8000`. No colima/docker touched.
- **Ingest:** `POST /v0/events?name=web_events`, 300 NDJSON rows → `{"quarantined_rows":0,"successful_rows":300}` (Tinybird-exact shape).
- **Query 1** `top_pages.json?limit=5` → full Tinybird envelope (`meta`/`data`/`rows`/`rows_before_limit_at_least`/`statistics`).
- **Query 2** `views_over_time.json?start=…` → `{{DateTime}}` param filters the series correctly.
- **Auth:** 401 no-token, 403 wrong-scope — parity model holds.

## Artifacts (in repo)

`examples/dashboards-demo/` — the 3 `.datasource`/`.pipe` files, `sample-events.ndjson`, `run.sh` (Apple container local; swap 2 lines for `docker compose` on prod), `README.md` with real receipts.

**USP draft:** `scratchpad/USP-draft.md` — honest coverage table (5/8 clean, 3 caveated), hero copy, "do not claim" list. Not placed on the site — yours to review + tell me the target repo/page.

## Found a real bug (demo surfaced it)

`tr deploy` can't bootstrap its own DB — native CH client pins session to `TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB`, so `CREATE DATABASE tr_main` fails `UNKNOWN_DATABASE` when absent. `run.sh` pre-creates as workaround. Fix = open bootstrap conn against `default`. Want me to file the issue?

`:8000` + 2 containers still up if you want to poke. Teardown: `container rm -f tr-ch tr-redis` + kill the `tr serve` (pid 78523).

{
  "command": "cd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny\ngh issue create -R ravencloak-org/tiny \\\n  --title \"tr deploy cannot bootstrap its own target database (UNKNOWN_DATABASE)\" \\\n  --milestone \"Phase 2 — API Publishing + Deployment\" \\\n  --label bug 2>/dev/null --body 'runDeploy opens the ClickHouse native client already scoped to the target DB, then tries to CREATE it on that same dead session. First deploy into a fresh workspace fails.\n\n## Repro\n```bash\n# fresh ClickHouse, database tr_main does not exist yet\ntr deploy --project-dir ./project --branch main\n```\n```\n→ workspace: main (database tr_main)\n✓ Validated 1 datasources, 2 pipes\n✓ Published 2 endpoints\nerror: create database tr_main: clickhouse exception 81: Code: 81.\n  DB::Exception: Database tr_main does not exist. (UNKNOWN_DATABASE)\n```\n`--check` passes (dry run skips the mutation); the real apply is what breaks.\n\n## Root cause\n`cmd/tr/deploy.go:57-62` builds the client with `Database: cfg.CHDatabase` (`tr_main`). The native session opens scoped to `tr_main`, which does not exist, so the subsequent `CreateDatabase(tr_main)` at `internal/deploy/deploy.go:133` runs against a database that is not there and errors `UNKNOWN_DATABASE`.\n\nThis contradicts the documented contract at `internal/clickhouse/ddl.go:277-279`: `CreateDatabase` \"runs against the client'\"'\"'s currently-scoped database, so the orchestrator calls it on a client pointed at an existing DB, then WithDatabase to target the new one.\" The CLI never points at an existing DB first — it targets the not-yet-created one. Ordering is inverted: it targets-then-creates instead of create-then-rescope.\n\n## Fix\nOpen the deploy client scoped to an existing DB (`default`, or empty), call `CreateDatabase(db)`, then re-scope onto `db` via `WithDatabase` (plumbing already exists — `internal/clickhouse/client.go:108`). Then run the diff/DDL.\n\n## Workaround\nPre-create the DB before first deploy:\n```bash\ncurl -s \"$CH_HTTP/\" -H \"X-ClickHouse-User: default\" -H \"X-ClickHouse-Key: $PW\" \\\n  --data-binary \"CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS tr_main\"\n```\nUsed in `examples/dashboards-demo/run.sh`.\n\n## Surfaced by\n`examples/dashboards-demo/` — first live drop-in demo (Tinybird dashboards use case). ADR 0006 (tr deploy).'",

}

===prod compose===
# One-click production stack for Dokploy: ClickHouse + Redis + TinyRaven.
# Dokploy → Create → Docker Compose → point at this file. It clones the repo,
# so relative paths resolve. Set the env in Dokploy (or a .env next to this).
#
# Local test:  docker compose -f deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml up -d
services:
  clickhouse:
    image: clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.3
    environment:
      CLICKHOUSE_DB: ${TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB:-tr_main}
      CLICKHOUSE_DEFAULT_ACCESS_MANAGEMENT: "1"
    ulimits:
      nofile: { soft: 262144, hard: 262144 }
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "--spider", "-q", "http://localhost:8123/ping"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 20
    volumes:
      - clickhouse_data:/var/lib/clickhouse
    restart: unless-stopped

  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine
    command: ["redis-server", "--appendonly", "yes"]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "ping"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 20
    volumes:
      - redis_data:/data
    restart: unless-stopped

  tinyraven:
    # Pinned to a version tag via deploy/.env (TINYRAVEN_TAG), bumped per release.
    # Distinct tags make Dokploy recreate reliably (a same :latest tag was skipped
    # on redeploy). pull_policy:always is belt-and-suspenders.
    image: ghcr.io/ravencloak-org/tiny:${TINYRAVEN_TAG:-latest}
    pull_policy: always
    command: ["serve"]
    # Host port defaults to an uncommon value to dodge collisions on shared
    # hosts (Dokploy/Traefik often hold 80/8000/8080). Override with TR_PORT.
    # With Dokploy domain routing, the host publish is optional — Traefik reaches
    # the container's internal :8000 directly.
    ports:
      - "${TR_PORT:-18000}:8000"
    environment:
      TR_HTTP_ADDR: ":8000"
      TR_CLICKHOUSE_HTTP: "http://clickhouse:8123"
      TR_CLICKHOUSE_NATIVE: "clickhouse:9000"
      TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB: "${TR_CLICKHOUSE_DB:-tr_main}"
      TR_REDIS_ADDR: "redis:6379"
      TR_PROJECT_DIR: "/project" # baked starter project; override with a volume
      TR_ADMIN_TOKEN: "${TR_ADMIN_TOKEN:?set a strong admin token}"
    depends_on:
      clickhouse: { condition: service_healthy }
      redis: { condition: service_healthy }
    restart: unless-stopped
    # Exposed via the host's cloudflared daemon: point that tunnel's ingress at
    # http://localhost:18000 (the published host port below) for tiny.ravencloak.org.

  # Marketing website (static Next.js export → nginx). Served at
  # tiny.ravencloak.org; point the host cloudflared tunnel ingress at
  # http://localhost:18080. The API lives separately at tiny-api.ravencloak.org.
  site:
    image: ghcr.io/ravencloak-org/tiny-site:${SITE_TAG:-latest}
    pull_policy: always
    ports:
      - "${SITE_PORT:-18080}:80"
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  clickhouse_data:
  redis_data:
===dokploy deploy workflow===
apt-publish.yml
ci.yml
goreleaser.yml
helm-publish.yml
release.yml
---
.github/workflows/release.yml
.github/workflows/goreleaser.yml
deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml
deploy/cloudflared/config.example.yml
deploy/.env
```

===release.yml deploy mechanism===
32:          # Single-arch x86 for prod/cloud deploy targets (no multi-arch/QEMU).
57:  deploy-dokploy:
61:      # Pins the Dokploy compose env to this release's VERSION tag, then deploys.
62:      # A version tag (not :latest) is a new image reference, so Dokploy always
63:      # pulls the new digest — the webhook+:latest path recreated from cache and
65:      # full (TR_ADMIN_TOKEN must be a repo secret). Skips cleanly if DOKPLOY_TOKEN
66:      # is unset. Requires the Dokploy panel reachable at DOKPLOY_URL.
67:      - name: Deploy to Dokploy (version-tagged)
69:          DOKPLOY_URL: https://dokploy.jobin.wtf
70:          DOKPLOY_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.DOKPLOY_TOKEN }}
75:          if [ -z "${DOKPLOY_TOKEN}" ]; then
76:            echo "DOKPLOY_TOKEN not set — skipping Dokploy deploy."
81:          echo "Pinning Dokploy compose env to ${TAG}..."
82:          jq -n --arg id "$COMPOSE_ID" --arg env "$env_blob" '{composeId:$id, env:$env}' \
83:            | curl -fsS -X POST "${DOKPLOY_URL}/api/compose.update" \
84:                -H "x-api-key: ${DOKPLOY_TOKEN}" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
86:          echo "Deploying ${TAG}..."
87:          curl -fsS -X POST "${DOKPLOY_URL}/api/compose.deploy" \
88:            -H "x-api-key: ${DOKPLOY_TOKEN}" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
89:            --data "$(jq -n --arg id "$COMPOSE_ID" '{composeId:$id}')" >/dev/null
90:          echo "Dokploy deploy triggered for ${TAG}."
===deploy/.env (mask secrets)===
# NOTE: Dokploy's stored env (panel/DB) is the SOURCE OF TRUTH for prod — it
# rewrites this file from the panel value on every deploy. Editing tags here
# alone does NOT change prod.
#
# Prod deploy model (2026-06-30): version-tagged via Dokploy API. On each tag,
# release.yml calls compose.update to set TINYRAVEN_TAG/SITE_TAG to the release
# version (a new image ref forces Dokploy to pull the new digest — :latest got
# served from cache and never updated), then compose.deploy. These values mirror
# the panel for local `docker compose` use.
TINYRAVEN_TAG=***
SITE_TAG=***
```

<!-- BEGIN:nextjs-agent-rules -->
# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing any code. Heed deprecation notices.
<!-- END:nextjs-agent-rules -->

########## app/page.tsx ##########
import { Benchmark } from "@/components/site/benchmark";
import { Features } from "@/components/site/features";
import { Footer } from "@/components/site/footer";
import { Hero } from "@/components/site/hero";
import { Migration } from "@/components/site/migration";
import { Nav } from "@/components/site/nav";
import { Pricing } from "@/components/site/pricing";

export default function Home() {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-1 flex-col">
      <Nav />
      <main className="flex-1">
        <Hero />
        <Features />
        <Migration />
        <Pricing />
        <Benchmark />
      </main>
      <Footer />
    </div>
  );
}

########## components/site/section.tsx ##########
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

interface SectionHeadingProps {
  eyebrow?: string;
  title: string;
  description?: string;
  className?: string;
  align?: "center" | "left";
}

export function SectionHeading({
  eyebrow,
  title,
  description,
  className,
  align = "center",
}: SectionHeadingProps) {
  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "max-w-2xl",
        align === "center" && "mx-auto text-center",
        className,
      )}
    >
      {eyebrow ? (
        <p className="text-sm font-medium uppercase tracking-widest text-violet-300/80">
          {eyebrow}
        </p>
      ) : null}
      <h2 className="mt-3 text-3xl font-semibold tracking-tight sm:text-4xl">
        {title}
      </h2>
      {description ? (
        <p className="mt-4 text-pretty text-base leading-relaxed text-zinc-400">
          {description}
        </p>
      ) : null}
    </div>
  );
}

########## components/site/features.tsx ##########
import type { ComponentType, SVGProps } from "react";
import {
  DatabaseIcon,
  FileCodeIcon,
  GitBranchIcon,
  LayersIcon,
  PlugIcon,
  ServerIcon,
} from "./icons";
import { SectionHeading } from "./section";

type Feature = {
  title: string;
  body: string;
  Icon: ComponentType<SVGProps<SVGSVGElement>>;
};

const features: Feature[] = [
  {
    title: "Drop-in API parity",
    body: "100% Tinybird API surface. Existing client code works by changing only TINYBIRD_HOST — no rewrites, no SDK swaps.",
    Icon: PlugIcon,
  },
  {
    title: ".datasource & .pipe files",
    body: "Define schemas and endpoints as plain files in your repo. Byte-compatible with Tinybird, versioned in Git as the source of truth.",
    Icon: FileCodeIcon,
  },
  {
    title: "Templated pipes → REST",
    body: "Write SQL with {{Type(param)}} templates. TinyRaven validates, escapes, and serves each pipe as a typed JSON REST endpoint.",
    Icon: DatabaseIcon,
  },
  {
    title: "Gatherer batching",
    body: "High-throughput ingest via a goroutine + channel that batches on max(N events, 5s) before flushing to ClickHouse. No tuning required.",
    Icon: LayersIcon,
  },
  {
    title: "tr deploy migrations",
    body: "Branch-aware deploys target one ClickHouse DB per Git branch. Breaking changes go shadow table → MV backfill → atomic EXCHANGE.",
    Icon: GitBranchIcon,
  },
  {
    title: "Self-hosted, your data",
    body: "Runs on your infra against OSS ClickHouse you control. No metering, no egress surprises, no vendor lock-in. Apache 2.0.",
    Icon: ServerIcon,
  },
];

export function Features() {
  return (
    <section id="features" className="mx-auto max-w-6xl scroll-mt-20 px-6 py-24">
      <SectionHeading
        eyebrow="What you get"
        title="Real-time analytics, on your terms"
        description="Everything the Tinybird workflow gives you — files, pipes, deploys — running as a single binary you own."
      />

      <div className="mt-14 grid gap-px overflow-hidden rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-white/10 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3">
        {features.map(({ title, body, Icon }) => (
          <div
            key={title}
            className="group bg-background p-7 transition-colors hover:bg-white/[0.03]"
          >
            <span className="grid h-10 w-10 place-items-center rounded-lg bg-violet-500/10 text-violet-300 ring-1 ring-violet-500/20 transition-colors group-hover:bg-violet-500/15">
              <Icon className="h-5 w-5" />
            </span>
            <h3 className="mt-5 text-base font-medium text-zinc-100">{title}</h3>
            <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-relaxed text-zinc-400">{body}</p>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>
    </section>
  );
}

import Link from "next/link";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { links } from "@/lib/data";
import { GitHubIcon } from "./icons";

const navItems = [
  { label: "Features", href: "#features" },
  { label: "Migration", href: "#migration" },
  { label: "Pricing", href: "#pricing" },
  { label: "Benchmark", href: "#benchmark" },
];

export function Nav() {
  return (
    <header className="sticky top-0 z-50 border-b border-white/5 bg-background/70 backdrop-blur-xl">
      <div className="mx-auto flex h-16 max-w-6xl items-center justify-between px-6">
        <Link href="#top" className="flex items-center gap-2.5">
          <span className="grid h-8 w-8 place-items-center rounded-lg bg-violet-500/15 text-violet-300 ring-1 ring-violet-500/30">
            <RavenMark className="h-5 w-5" />
          </span>
          <span className="text-[15px] font-semibold tracking-tight">
            Tiny<span className="text-violet-300">Raven</span>
          </span>
        </Link>

        <nav className="hidden items-center gap-7 md:flex">
          {navItems.map((item) => (
            <Link
              key={item.href}
              href={item.href}
              className="text-sm text-zinc-400 transition-colors hover:text-zinc-100"
            >
              {item.label}
            </Link>
          ))}
        </nav>

        <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
          <Button
            render={<a href={links.github} target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" />}
            size="sm"
            className="bg-white text-zinc-900 hover:bg-zinc-200"
          >
            <GitHubIcon className="h-4 w-4" />
            GitHub
          </Button>
        </div>
      </div>
    </header>
  );
}

function RavenMark({ className }: { className?: string }) {
  return (
    <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="currentColor" aria-hidden className={className}>
      <path d="M3 6c4 0 7 1.5 9 4 1.2-1 2.8-1.5 4.5-1.5L21 5l-1.5 4c.9 1 1.5 2.4 1.5 4 0 4-3.5 7-9 7-3 0-5.4-1-7-2.6 1.2.3 2.4.2 3.4-.3C5 18 3.5 15.5 3.5 12c0-.8.1-1.5.3-2.2C3.3 9 3 7.7 3 6Zm12 4.5a1 1 0 1 0 0 2 1 1 0 0 0 0-2Z" />
    </svg>
  );
}

===code-block===
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

interface CodeLine {
  text: string;
  // visual treatment for diff-style blocks
  variant?: "default" | "added" | "removed" | "comment";
}

interface CodeBlockProps {
  lines: CodeLine[];
  label?: string;
  className?: string;
}

const variantClass: Record<NonNullable<CodeLine["variant"]>, string> = {
  default: "text-zinc-200",
  added: "text-emerald-300",
  removed: "text-rose-300/70 line-through decoration-rose-400/40",
  comment: "text-zinc-500",
};

export function CodeBlock({ lines, label, className }: CodeBlockProps) {
  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "overflow-hidden rounded-xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/80 shadow-2xl shadow-black/40 backdrop-blur",
        className,
      )}
    >
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2 border-b border-white/5 px-4 py-2.5">
        <span className="h-3 w-3 rounded-full bg-rose-500/70" />
        <span className="h-3 w-3 rounded-full bg-amber-400/70" />
        <span className="h-3 w-3 rounded-full bg-emerald-500/70" />
        {label ? (
          <span className="ml-2 font-mono text-xs text-zinc-500">{label}</span>
        ) : null}
      </div>
      <pre className="overflow-x-auto px-4 py-4 font-mono text-[13px] leading-relaxed">
        <code>
          {lines.map((line, i) => (
            <div key={i} className={variantClass[line.variant ?? "default"]}>
              {line.text || " "}
            </div>
          ))}
        </code>
      </pre>
    </div>
  );
}

// Single source of truth for all pricing and benchmark numbers shown on the
// marketing site. The orchestrator overwrites `benchmark` with real load-test
// figures — keep this module the only place these numbers live.

export const benchmark = {
  // TinyRaven figure is REAL: measured locally on Apple Silicon (M-series),
  // 50 concurrent clients, batched NDJSON, 15s — 2,656,000 events ingested and
  // persisted to ClickHouse. ~177k events/s, p50 14ms / p95 71ms.
  tinyraven: { throughput: 176935, p95ms: 71, note: "1 node, local M-series, batched ingest → ClickHouse (measured)" },
  // Tinybird figure is ILLUSTRATIVE for comparison only — not a measured
  // head-to-head (no Tinybird account was used). Throughput on a managed plan
  // varies by tier/region.
  tinybird: { throughput: 150000, p95ms: 60, note: "managed; illustrative, not measured" },
  source: "TinyRaven: local load test (50 clients, batched, 1 node) — measured. Tinybird: illustrative.",
} as const;

export const pricing = {
  tinyraven: {
    plan: "Self-hosted (Apache 2.0)",
    monthly: "infra only (~$60 t3.large)",
    events: "unbounded (your CH)",
    retention: "your disk",
  },
  tinybird: {
    plan: "Tinybird managed",
    monthly: "usage-based ($$$)",
    events: "metered",
    retention: "plan-limited",
  },
} as const;

// Convenience rows for the comparison table, derived from `pricing` so the
// table never hardcodes its own copies of these values.
export const pricingRows = [
  { label: "Plan", tinyraven: pricing.tinyraven.plan, tinybird: pricing.tinybird.plan },
  { label: "Monthly cost", tinyraven: pricing.tinyraven.monthly, tinybird: pricing.tinybird.monthly },
  { label: "Events", tinyraven: pricing.tinyraven.events, tinybird: pricing.tinybird.events },
  { label: "Retention", tinyraven: pricing.tinyraven.retention, tinybird: pricing.tinybird.retention },
] as const;

===CHANGELOG head===
# Changelog

All notable changes to TinyRaven are documented here. Format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/); this project adheres to
[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [0.3.2] — 2026-06-30

### Changed
- Auto-deploy on release is now **version-tagged via the Dokploy API** (`compose.update` env → `compose.deploy`) instead of a webhook on `:latest`. A version tag is a new image reference, so Dokploy pulls the new digest every time; the `:latest` webhook recreated from cache and silently never updated.

## [0.3.1] — 2026-06-30

### Added
- API read endpoints toward full Tinybird `/v0` parity: `GET /v0/datasources`, `GET /v0/datasources/{name}`, `GET /v0/pipes`, `GET /v0/pipes/{name}` (all ADMIN), plus pipe output formats `GET /v0/pipes/{name}.{csv,ndjson}` (READ-scoped) alongside the existing `.json`.
- Distribution: signed APT + RPM repos published to GitHub Pages (`apt-get install tinyraven`); GoReleaser Scoop/AUR/Nix/winget publisher blocks (fail-safe — push only when their token exists); install matrix + `docs/install.md`.
- `docs/parity-gaps.md` ranked `/v0` parity audit.

### Changed
- Prod deploy model: image tags pinned to `latest`; release builds `:version` + `:latest` then triggers the Dokploy deploy webhook (`pull_policy: always` recreates on the new digest). Dokploy panel env is the documented source of truth.
- APT/RPM gh-pages publish now does a scoped `rsync --delete` of `apt/`+`rpm/` (keep-2 pool prune actually removes old blobs); `helm-publish` + `apt-publish` share a concurrency group to avoid gh-pages races.

### Tests
- `internal/apierr` and `internal/model` 0→100%; `internal/pipe` 86.7→96% (error-envelope mapping, `Token.HasScope` auth gate, registry hot-reload swap, control-flow branch + non-boolean-condition rejection).

> AGENT

Root cause: stock `nginx:alpine`, no `try_files` — `/use-cases` hits the RSC dir, 301s to `/use-cases/`, no index → 403. Fix = nginx conf with `try_files $uri $uri.html` (serves `use-cases.html`, kills the redirect+scheme-drop too). Adding conf, testing locally before re-tagging.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01Q8dw1HzrrUG3Sj8ZX5T9aJ
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny/site/nginx.conf",
  "content": "server {\n    listen 80;\n    server_name _;\n    root /usr/share/nginx/html;\n    index index.html;\n\n    # Next static export writes /use-cases.html (not /use-cases/index.html).\n    # $uri.html serves it directly for extensionless routes — no dir redirect,\n    # so no 301 (which also dropped https→http behind Cloudflare).\n    location / {\n        try_files $uri $uri.html $uri/ =404;\n    }\n\n    error_page 404 /404.html;\n}\n"
}
```

===docs: ADR 0030 token value===
# Resource tokens: declarative materialization on deploy, orphans warned not deleted
ADR 0005 says resource-scoped tokens are "declared in `.pipe`/`.datasource` and materialized on `tr deploy`." This pins down the flow.
- **Declaration (parity):** a `TOKEN "name" READ` line inside a `.pipe` (grants `PIPE:READ` on that pipe) or `.datasource` (`READ` / `APPEND`). The token **name** lives in git; the token **value** never does.
- **Materialize on deploy — idempotent upsert.** For each declared name, `tr deploy` looks it up in Redis (ADR 0001): absent → generate an opaque value (ADR 0005), store `value→scopes` + a `name→value` index, print the value **once** in deploy output. Present → keep the existing value. A redeploy **never rotates** a token's value — rotation would break every live client holding it.
- **Scopes are declarative — union across files.** A name declared in several files (`TOKEN "dashboard" READ` in pipe A and pipe B) is one token whose scope is the union of all its declarations, recomputed and re-applied on every deploy. Editing a file changes scope; the value is stable.
- **Value surface:** Redis holds the value; `tr token ls` and deploy output print it. Never written to git.
- **File-managed vs manual is a hard split.** Deploy reconciles **only** file-declared tokens. The bootstrap `ADMIN` token and anything from `tr token create` are manual — deploy never narrows, rotates, or prunes them.
- **Orphans are warned, not deleted.** A token whose name is declared in no file after a deploy is **orphaned**: deploy prints `token "X" no longer declared — run 'tr token rm X' to revoke` and **leaves it working**. Actual deletion is an explicit act: `tr token rm <name>` or `tr deploy --prune-tokens`.
- **Auto-revoke orphans (deploy deletes the Redis key when the declaration disappears)** — rejected as the default. It is "clean" and matches ADR 0005's instant-revocation, but it makes a typo or an accidental line deletion silently kill a live integration on the next deploy. Token deletion is destructive and should be a deliberate command, not a side effect of editing a file.
- **Rotate the value on every deploy** — rejected. Breaks all existing holders of the token; defeats the purpose of a stable resource token.
```

===API Dockerfile (final stage base)===
# Multi-stage build for the `tr` binary.
FROM golang:1.26-alpine AS build
WORKDIR /src
COPY go.mod go.sum ./
RUN go mod download
COPY . .
ARG VERSION=dev
RUN CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -ldflags "-s -w -X main.version=${VERSION}" -o /out/tr ./cmd/tr

FROM alpine:3.20
RUN apk add --no-cache ca-certificates
COPY --from=build /out/tr /usr/local/bin/tr
# Ship a starter project so a fresh deploy has a working datasource + pipe.
# Replace /project (bind mount or your own COPY) with your real .datasource/.pipe.
COPY examples/quickstart /project
ENV TR_PROJECT_DIR=/project
EXPOSE 8000
ENTRYPOINT ["tr"]
CMD ["serve"]

> AGENT

Seed live on `main` (300 rows). Here's the one block — run it on the **Dokploy host** (SSH in). It execs into the container, deploys the demo, seeds, and prints the read-only token:

```bash
CID=$(docker ps --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -i tinyraven | head -1)
echo "container: $CID"
docker exec -i "$CID" sh <<'SCRIPT'
set -e
apk add --no-cache curl >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
mkdir -p /tmp/demo
cat > /tmp/demo/web_events.datasource <<'EOF'
SCHEMA >
    timestamp DateTime,
    user_id String,
    path String,
    country String,
    device String

ENGINE "MergeTree"
ENGINE_SORTING_KEY "(path, timestamp)"
ENGINE_PARTITION_KEY "toYYYYMM(timestamp)"
EOF
cat > /tmp/demo/top_pages.pipe <<'EOF'
NODE endpoint
SQL >
    SELECT path, count() AS views, uniq(user_id) AS visitors
    FROM web_events
    GROUP BY path
    ORDER BY views DESC
    LIMIT {{Int32(limit, 10)}}
TYPE endpoint
EOF
cat > /tmp/demo/views_over_time.pipe <<'EOF'
NODE endpoint
SQL >
    SELECT toStartOfHour(timestamp) AS hour, count() AS views
    FROM web_events
    WHERE timestamp >= {{DateTime(start, '2020-01-01 00:00:00')}}
    GROUP BY hour
    ORDER BY hour
TYPE endpoint
EOF

tr deploy --project-dir /tmp/demo --branch main

curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ravencloak-org/tiny/main/examples/dashboards-demo/sample-events.ndjson -o /tmp/seed.ndjson
curl -s -X POST "http://localhost:8000/v0/events?name=web_events" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TR_ADMIN_TOKEN" --data-binary @/tmp/seed.ndjson; echo

echo "=== DEMO READ TOKEN (paste this back) ==="
tr token create --name demo_ro --read top_pages --read views_over_time | grep tr_
SCRIPT
```

7	  originSessionId: 8800a864-e1cb-4d13-9744-9d16ef2a83c7

9	
10	TinyRaven runs in prod on a Vultr VPS (`root@139.84.219.81`, key `~/.ssh/id_ed25519`) via **Dokploy** (a Docker Compose app from `deploy/docker-compose.prod.yml`). Stack: `site` (nginx static, host port 18080) + `tinyraven` (API, host port 18000) + ClickHouse 26.3 + Redis (named volumes, persistent).
11	
12	**Public hosts (Cloudflare):**
13	- `https://tiny.ravencloak.org` → marketing **website** (→ localhost:18080)
14	- `https://tiny-api.ravencloak.org` → **API**, this is `TINYBIRD_HOST` (→ localhost:18000)
15	
16	**Tunnel:** exposed by the **caw** cloudflared tunnel (`c3308fe1-…`), managed by **systemd** `cloudflared-caw.service`, config `/root/.cloudflared/config.yml`. That one tunnel also serves caw.ravencloak.org + the viewrr.stream hosts — **be careful editing its config**. A second tunnel `dokploy` (`cloudflared-dokploy.service`, `dokploy.yml`) serves dokploy.jobin.wtf.
17	
18	**Hard-won gotchas:**
19	- **cloudflared here EXITS on SIGHUP (does not hot-reload).** Plus `Restart=on-failure` ignores a clean exit. So `kill -HUP` took the tunnel DOWN. To apply config changes: edit config.yml then **`systemctl restart cloudflared-caw`** — never SIGHUP.
20	- **Cloudflare Universal SSL covers first-level subdomains only** (`*.ravencloak.org`). Second-level like `api.tiny.ravencloak.org` → TLS handshake failure (needs paid ACM). That's why the API is `tiny-api.` not `api.tiny.`.
21	- `pgrep -f "run caw"` matches your own ssh shell (false positive) — use `pgrep -x cloudflared` / `ps -C cloudflared`.
22	- The API has **no `/` route** (API-first) → `/` returns 404 by design; health is `/health` (NOT `/ready`).
23	- **Cloudflare bot-fight returns `403 error code: 1010` to non-browser User-Agents** (python urllib, curl libs). Even unauthed `/health` gets 1010. Fix when scripting against prod: send a real browser `User-Agent` header. Real Tinybird SDK clients may trip this → may need a CF UA/WAF allowlist rule.
24	
25	**Dokploy env is the source of truth — it CLOBBERS repo `deploy/.env`:** Dokploy stores the compose env in its postgres (`docker exec dokploy-postgres.* psql -U dokploy -d dokploy`, table `compose`, row `name='backend'`, column `env`). At **every** deploy it rewrites `/etc/dokploy/compose/tiny-backend-btyefk/code/deploy/.env` from that DB value, overwriting whatever git committed. So the repo `deploy/.env` version-pin is **cosmetic** — prod silently reverted to an old `TINYRAVEN_TAG` once because the DB env still pinned it. **Version bumps + secrets must be changed in the Dokploy DB env (or UI), not repo `.env`.** Edit DB durably via `UPDATE compose SET env=convert_from(decode('<base64>','base64'),'UTF8') WHERE name='backend'`, then recreate: `cd code/deploy && DOCKER_CONFIG=/root/.docker docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml -p tiny-backend-btyefk up -d --no-deps --pull always tinyraven site`. **Always pass `-f docker-compose.prod.yml`** — bare `docker compose up` walks to the root dev compose ("no such service: site").
26	
27	**Token rotation needs TWO places:** the admin token persists in **both** Dokploy DB env (`TR_ADMIN_TOKEN`) **and** Redis under `tr:token:<value>`. Restarting with a new env token only ADDS the new key; the old one keeps validating until you `redis-cli DEL tr:token:<oldvalue>` (redis container `tiny-backend-btyefk-redis-1`, no auth). Datasource registry keys are `tr:ds:<name>`. Current admin token saved root-only at `/root/.tinyraven_admin_token` (prod) + `~/.config/tinyraven/prod-admin.env` (local).
28	
29	**Images:** `ghcr.io/ravencloak-org/{tiny,tiny-site}:vX.Y.Z` + `:latest`, **public**, **linux/amd64 only** (user wants x86; local M-series builds arm64 itself).
30	
31	**Auto-deploy on release (WORKING, version-tagged Dokploy API):** push tag `vX.Y.Z` → `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds both images, creates GH release, then the `deploy-dokploy` job POSTs the Dokploy API: `compose.update` (sets env `TINYRAVEN_TAG`/`SITE_TAG`=tag, rebuilt from secrets) then `compose.deploy`. A **version** tag is a new image ref → Dokploy always pulls the new digest. **The webhook+`:latest` path FAILED** — Dokploy's webhook redeploy recreates from the *cached* `:latest` and never pulls (even with `pull_policy: always`), so prod silently stayed on the old version. Version-tag is the fix.
32	- Dokploy panel/API: **`https://dokploy.jobin.wtf`** (public; auth header `x-api-key: <key>`). composeId **`HHL9RZlnr5lErt91OJjwQ`** (name `backend`). Routes: `compose.update` (REPLACES the whole `env` blob — must resend `TR_ADMIN_TOKEN`+tags), `compose.deploy`, `compose.one`, `compose.all`.
33	- GH secrets: `DOKPLOY_TOKEN` (Dokploy api key — better-auth stores it HASHED in DB table `apikey.key`, NOT recoverable), `TR_ADMIN_TOKEN` (so CI can rebuild the env blob). `DOKPLOY_DEPLOY_WEBHOOK` now unused.
34	- Manual deploy still works: set DB env tag (see above) + `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml -p tiny-backend-btyefk up -d --no-deps --pull always tinyraven site`.
35	
36	See [[parallel-agent-orchestration]].

> DEVELOPER

also why no timescale db, since clickhouse is olap db we can use timescale db to cache any timescale data for faster returns right? most of these timescale db doesnt change just keeps on appending like WAL

> AGENT

Short answer: Timescale would make it **slower**, not faster — and the caching you're describing already exists two ways. Premise is backwards.

**ClickHouse already IS the fast append-only time-series store.** It's columnar OLAP built for exactly this: append-heavy, immutable, `GROUP BY toStartOfHour(...)` scans. TimescaleDB is Postgres-with-hypertables — row-oriented at heart, compression bolted on. On the dashboard query shape (large-scan aggregations), CH beats Timescale in basically every public benchmark. Putting Timescale "in front" of CH to speed up reads caches a fast thing behind a slower thing.

**The "cache append-only rollups" job is already done by materialized views.** That's the native mechanism. MV pre-aggregates on ingest (incremental) → the dashboard hits a tiny pre-rolled table, not the raw 300M rows. Append-only data that "keeps appending like WAL" → MV rolls each new part forward automatically. `.pipe` files support `MATERIALIZATION` / `TARGET_TABLE` for exactly this. No second DB.

**Repeated identical queries → Redis result cache.** Already the design: Redis holds token validation, rate-limit counters, **and query results** (pipes carry `CACHE_TTL`). Hot dashboard query served from Redis, skips CH entirely.

**Why not add Timescale anyway:**
- Constraint: **no PostgreSQL** (ADR 0001, Redis-only metadata). Timescale = dragging a whole Postgres in.
- Doubles the data + a sync pipeline (CH↔Timescale), two sources of truth, replication lag → dashboards show stale/inconsistent numbers.
- Tinybird itself is ClickHouse-only. Adding Timescale breaks the parity thesis (ScyllaDB→Cassandra, same engine).
- Ops: a second stateful system to back up, tune, fail over. For zero read-speed win.

So the caching ladder is already: **MV (pre-aggregate) → Redis (result TTL) → CH (raw)**. Timescale slots nowhere better than what's there. If a specific query is slow, the fix is an MV or a Redis TTL, not a new database.

Want me to add a short "why not a second DB / caching model" note to the ADRs or the use-cases page? And — the v0.3.5 site deploy is still finishing; I'll confirm the live links when it lands.

===format of a recent ADR===
# Redis is the only metadata store; no PostgreSQL

TinyRaven stores all metadata — workspace state, pipe/datasource definitions, tokens, and deploy state — in **Redis** (AOF-persisted, mounted volume). ClickHouse holds the actual event data. There is no PostgreSQL, SQLite, or pluggable-backend matrix.

## Why

- **Tinybird does exactly this.** Tinybird uses Redis for workspace/pipe/token metadata and ClickHouse for data — no relational metadata DB. Matching it serves the parity goal and is a proven design.
- **Zero new dependency.** Redis is already in the stack (token cache, rate limiting). Postgres would add a second stateful service to every deploy path (Heroku add-on, AWS RDS, Compose/Helm container) — directly undercutting the "minimal infra / pay only infrastructure" positioning.
- **Git is the source of truth for definitions.** `.datasource` and `.pipe` files live in the user's repo. The metadata store is a *registry of what has been deployed*, rebuildable by re-running `tr deploy`. So definitions don't need transactional durability — only tokens and deploy state do, which Redis persistence covers.

## Consequences

- Redis runs as a **system of record**, not a throwaway cache: AOF persistence + a mounted volume are mandatory in every deploy template.
- No cross-store SQL transaction during `tr deploy`. Partial-deploy failures are handled by idempotent re-apply (re-run `tr deploy` from git), not rollback. ClickHouse DDL isn't transactional anyway, so this changes nothing in practice.

## Considered and rejected

- **PostgreSQL (prod) + SQLite (local) split** — two backends to build/test/document for an MVP; and the "local needs no server" premise fails because `tr local start` already runs Redis + ClickHouse in Compose.
- **ParadeDB** — a Postgres BM25/search extension; no metadata workload here needs full-text search.
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny/docs/adr/0034-no-second-store-caching-via-mv-and-redis.md",
  "content": "# No second analytical/time-series store; read acceleration is materialized views + Redis\n\nTinyRaven does not add TimescaleDB, DuckDB, a search engine, or any second store to \"speed up\" or \"cache\" reads in front of ClickHouse. Read acceleration has exactly two layers, both already in the stack:\n\n1. **Materialized views** — pre-aggregate on ingest. Append-only event data rolls forward incrementally into small target tables; dashboard queries hit the rollup, not the raw rows. Declared in `.pipe` files (`MATERIALIZATION` / `TARGET_TABLE`).\n2. **Redis result cache** — repeated identical pipe queries served from Redis with a per-pipe `CACHE_TTL`, skipping ClickHouse entirely.\n\nLadder: **MV (pre-aggregate) → Redis (result TTL) → ClickHouse (raw)**.\n\n## Why\n\n- **ClickHouse is already the fast append-only time-series store.** Columnar OLAP built for `GROUP BY toStartOfHour(...)` over large immutable scans. TimescaleDB is Postgres-with-hypertables — row-oriented at heart; on the dashboard aggregation shape it loses to ClickHouse in public benchmarks. Putting Timescale \"in front\" of CH caches a fast store behind a slower one.\n- **The append-only-rollup job is what materialized views are for.** Data that \"keeps appending like a WAL and never changes\" is the ideal MV input — each new part rolls forward automatically. That is the cache; it needs no second database.\n- **A second store doubles the data and adds a sync pipeline.** CH↔Timescale replication means two sources of truth and lag — dashboards would show stale/inconsistent numbers. A result cache (Redis, TTL-bounded) has bounded, understood staleness; a mirrored DB does not.\n- **No PostgreSQL (ADR 0001).** Timescale is a Postgres extension — it drags a whole Postgres into every deploy path, undercutting the minimal-infra positioning.\n- **Parity.** Tinybird is ClickHouse-only (its speed-up primitive is materialized pipes, not a second engine). Matching that keeps the drop-in thesis intact.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- A slow pipe is fixed by adding a materialized view or a `CACHE_TTL`, never by introducing a new backend.\n- The only stateful services remain ClickHouse (data) and Redis (metadata + hot cache) — see ADR 0001.\n\n## Considered and rejected\n\n- **TimescaleDB as a read cache / mirror** — slower than CH for these queries, adds Postgres (violates ADR 0001), introduces a sync pipeline and a second source of truth for zero read-speed gain.\n- **DuckDB / embedded OLAP as a query-side accelerator** — no workload here that CH + MV + Redis doesn't already cover; adds a second query engine to reason about.\n"
}

> AGENT

Two blockers shape this:
1. **Site already has a polished animated visx bar-chart** (`components/charts/bar-chart.tsx`) — reusable.
2. **API sends no CORS headers** — a browser fetch from `tiny.` → `tiny-api.` gets blocked. So a *live* in-browser dashboard needs CORS added to the pipe endpoints (small server change + redeploy). Tinybird sets CORS on pipes for exactly this — it's a real parity gap, worth fixing.

One fork before I build:

===server.go pipe routes + middleware wiring===
	r.Use(chimw.RequestID)
	r.Use(chimw.Recoverer)
	if deps.MetricsMiddleware != nil {
		r.Use(deps.MetricsMiddleware)
	}

	// Unauthenticated: health (ADR 0024) + metrics scrape endpoint.
	r.Get("/health", s.handleLiveness)
	r.Get("/health/ready", s.handleReadiness)
	if deps.MetricsHandler != nil {
		r.Handle("/v0/metrics", deps.MetricsHandler)
	}

	// /tr/v1 — TinyRaven-native namespace (ADR 0029). Docs UI is off by default
	// (ADR 0017); when on, the UI + an unauthenticated spec it can fetch are
	// served here (the /v0 spec stays bearer-gated for API clients).
	if deps.DocsEnabled && deps.DocsUI != nil && deps.OpenAPI != nil {
		r.Handle("/tr/v1/docs", deps.DocsUI)
		r.Get("/tr/v1/openapi.json", s.handleOpenAPI)
	}

	// /v0 — frozen Tinybird mirror (ADR 0029), behind bearer auth.
	r.Route("/v0", func(r chi.Router) {
		r.Use(s.authMiddleware)
		r.Post("/events", s.handleEvents)
		if deps.SQLProxy != nil {
			// Raw SQL is powerful -> ADMIN only (ADR 0011 + 0005).
			r.With(s.adminOnly).Handle("/sql", deps.SQLProxy) // GET + POST
		}
		if deps.Datasources != nil {
			// Token-scope-filtered listing (Tinybird parity): each token sees the
			// datasources it can READ; ADMIN sees all. The handlers do the filtering.
			r.Get("/datasources", s.handleListDatasources)
			r.Get("/datasources/{name}", s.handleGetDatasource)
		}
		if deps.PipeReg != nil {
			// Scope-filtered pipe introspection (Tinybird parity): READ:<pipe> sees
			// that pipe; ADMIN sees all. Note: {name} must not swallow the
			// .json/.csv/.ndjson/.parquet data routes; chi matches the static suffix
			// first.
			r.Get("/pipes", s.handleListPipes)
			r.Get("/pipes/{name}", s.handleGetPipe)
			// On-demand copy trigger: runs INSERT INTO <target> SELECT <pipe SQL>.
			// ADMIN or APPEND:<target> (checked in the handler, which resolves the
			// target from the pipe). Needs both the runner and the registry.
			if deps.CopyRunner != nil {
				r.Post("/pipes/{name}/copy", s.handleCopyPipe)
			}
		}
		if deps.OpenAPI != nil {
			r.Get("/openapi.json", s.handleOpenAPI)
		}
		// Pipe reads (data path) carry the per-token rate limiter (ADR 0015). Each
		// format is a distinct static-suffix route; only the CH FORMAT + content
		// type differ (READ:<pipe> scoped).
		r.Group(func(r chi.Router) {
			if deps.RateLimit != nil {
				r.Use(deps.RateLimit)
			}
			r.Get("/pipes/{name}.json", s.handlePipe(model.FormatJSON))
			r.Get("/pipes/{name}.csv", s.handlePipe(model.FormatCSV))
			r.Get("/pipes/{name}.ndjson", s.handlePipe(model.FormatNDJSON))
			r.Get("/pipes/{name}.parquet", s.handlePipe(model.FormatParquet))
		})
	})

	return r
}

// handleOpenAPI serves the runtime-generated OpenAPI spec (ADR 0017).
func (s *server) handleOpenAPI(w http.ResponseWriter, _ *http.Request) {

===BarChartProps===
export interface BarChartProps {
  /** Data array - each item should have an x-axis key and numeric values */
  data: Record<string, unknown>[];
  /** Key in data for the categorical axis. Default: "name" */
  xDataKey?: string;
  /** Chart margins */
  margin?: Partial<Margin>;
  /** Animation duration in milliseconds. Default: 1100 */
  animationDuration?: number;
  /** CSS easing for bar grow transitions. */
  animationEasing?: string;
  /** Motion enter transition (spring or cubic-bezier tween). */
  enterTransition?: Transition;
  /** Signature of motion URL state — triggers enter replay when it changes. */
  revealSignature?: string;
  /** Aspect ratio as "width / height". Default: "2 / 1" */
  aspectRatio?: string;
  /** Additional class name for the container */
  className?: string;
  /** Gap between bar groups as a fraction of band width (0-1). Default: 0.2 */
  barGap?: number;
  /** Fixed bar width in pixels. If not set, bars auto-size to fill the band. */
  barWidth?: number;
  /** Bar chart orientation. Default: "vertical" */
  orientation?: BarOrientation;
  /** Whether to stack bars instead of grouping them. Default: false */
  stacked?: boolean;
  /** Gap between stacked bar segments in pixels. Default: 0 */
  stackGap?: number;
  /** Child components (Bar, Grid, ChartTooltip, etc.). Optional — omit for a
   * pure `status="loading"` skeleton. */
  children?: ReactNode;
  /** Reports reveal lifecycle for OG screenshots and loading orchestration. */
  onPhaseChange?: (phase: ChartPhase) => void;
  /** Fetch / display status. When `"loading"`, a shimmer skeleton replaces the
   * bars (no chart data required). Default: `"ready"`. */
  status?: ChartStatus;
}

const DEFAULT_MARGIN: Margin = { top: 40, right: 40, bottom: 40, left: 40 };

// Extract bar configs from children synchronously
function extractBarConfigs(children: ReactNode): LineConfig[] {
  const configs: LineConfig[] = [];

  forEachChartChild(children, (child) => {
    const childType = child.type as {
      displayName?: string;
      name?: string;
      __isBarDepthLayer?: boolean;
    };
    // Bar-depth surface layers (BarDepthBack/Front, BarPulse) carry a
    // `dataKey` to pair with a Bar but are not series themselves — skip them
    // so they don't inflate the series count and shrink the real bars.
    if (childType.__isBarDepthLayer) {
      return;
    }
    const componentName =
      typeof child.type === "function"
        ? childType.displayName || childType.name || ""
        : "";

    const props = child.props as BarProps | undefined;
    const isBarComponent =
      componentName === "Bar" ||
      (props && typeof props.dataKey === "string" && props.dataKey.length > 0);

    if (isBarComponent && props?.dataKey) {
      // Use stroke for tooltip dot color if provided, otherwise fall back to fill
      // This allows gradient/pattern fills to have a solid dot color
      const dotColor =
        props.stroke || props.fill || "var(--chart-line-primary)";
      configs.push({
        dataKey: props.dataKey,
        stroke: dotColor,
        strokeWidth: 0,
        yAxisId: props.yAxisId,
      });
    }
  });

  return configs;
}

"use client";

import { Bar } from "@/components/charts/bar";
import { BarChart } from "@/components/charts/bar-chart";
import { BarXAxis } from "@/components/charts/bar-x-axis";
import { BarYAxis } from "@/components/charts/bar-y-axis";
import { Grid } from "@/components/charts/grid";
import { benchmark } from "@/lib/data";
import { SectionHeading } from "./section";

const throughputData = [
  { name: "TinyRaven", value: benchmark.tinyraven.throughput },
  { name: "Tinybird", value: benchmark.tinybird.throughput },
];

const latencyData = [
  { name: "TinyRaven", value: benchmark.tinyraven.p95ms },
  { name: "Tinybird", value: benchmark.tinybird.p95ms },
];

export function Benchmark() {
  return (
    <section
      id="benchmark"
      className="relative scroll-mt-20 border-t border-white/5 bg-white/[0.02] py-24"
    >
      <div className="mx-auto max-w-6xl px-6">
        <SectionHeading
          eyebrow="Benchmark"
          title="Parity on performance, too"
          description="Throughput and p95 latency, measured side by side. TinyRaven keeps pace with managed Tinybird on commodity hardware."
        />

        <div className="mt-14 grid gap-6 lg:grid-cols-2">
          <ChartCard
            title="Throughput"
            unit="events / sec"
            data={throughputData}
            fill="#a78bfa"
            format={(v) => `${(v / 1000).toFixed(0)}k`}
            note={`${benchmark.tinyraven.throughput.toLocaleString()} ev/s · ${benchmark.tinyraven.note}`}
          />
          <ChartCard
            title="p95 latency"
            unit="milliseconds (lower is better)"
            data={latencyData}
            fill="#818cf8"
            format={(v) => `${v} ms`}
            note={`TinyRaven ${benchmark.tinyraven.p95ms} ms vs Tinybird ${benchmark.tinybird.p95ms} ms`}
          />
        </div>

        <p className="mt-8 text-center text-xs text-zinc-500">
          Source: {benchmark.source}
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>
  );
}

interface ChartCardProps {
  title: string;
  unit: string;
  data: { name: string; value: number }[];
  fill: string;
  format: (v: number) => string;
  note: string;
}

function ChartCard({ title, unit, data, fill, format, note }: ChartCardProps) {
  return (
    <div className="rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/40 p-6">
      <div className="flex items-baseline justify-between">
        <h3 className="text-base font-medium text-zinc-100">{title}</h3>
        <span className="text-xs text-zinc-500">{unit}</span>
      </div>

      <div className="mt-2">
        <BarChart
          data={data}
          xDataKey="name"
          aspectRatio="16 / 9"
          barGap={0.45}
          margin={{ top: 24, right: 16, bottom: 28, left: 44 }}
        >
          <Grid horizontal numTicksRows={4} />
          <Bar dataKey="value" fill={fill} lineCap={6} />
          <BarXAxis />
          <BarYAxis maxLabels={5} />
        </BarChart>
      </div>

      <div className="mt-4 grid grid-cols-2 gap-3">
        {data.map((d, i) => (
          <div
            key={d.name}
            className="rounded-lg border border-white/5 bg-white/[0.02] px-3 py-2"
          >
            <div className="flex items-center gap-2 text-xs text-zinc-400">
              <span
                className="h-2 w-2 rounded-full"
                style={{ background: i === 0 ? fill : "#52525b" }}
              />
              {d.name}
            </div>
            <div className="mt-1 font-mono text-lg font-semibold text-zinc-100">
              {format(d.value)}
            </div>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>

      <p className="mt-4 text-xs text-zinc-500">{note}</p>
    </div>
  );
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny/internal/api/middleware.go",
  "old_string": "// authMiddleware validates the bearer token (or ?token= query param for browser\n// embedding, ADR 0025) against the TokenStore and stashes it in the context.",
  "new_string": "// corsMiddleware makes responses readable cross-origin so browser dashboards\n// can call pipe endpoints directly (Tinybird parity — pipes are CORS-open for\n// public read tokens; ADR 0025). Auth is unchanged: `*` grants read visibility,\n// not access — a valid token is still required. Preflight OPTIONS short-circuits\n// here, before authMiddleware, so it isn't rejected for lacking a token.\nfunc corsMiddleware(next http.Handler) http.Handler {\n\treturn http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\", \"*\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Access-Control-Allow-Methods\", \"GET, POST, OPTIONS\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Access-Control-Allow-Headers\", \"Authorization, Content-Type\")\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Access-Control-Max-Age\", \"86400\")\n\t\tif r.Method == http.MethodOptions {\n\t\t\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusNoContent)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tnext.ServeHTTP(w, r)\n\t})\n}\n\n// authMiddleware validates the bearer token (or ?token= query param for browser\n// embedding, ADR 0025) against the TokenStore and stashes it in the context."
}

{
  "command": "cd /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/tiny\ngo build ./... 2>&1 | tail -10 && echo \"BUILD OK\"\n# quick smoke: does the top router now emit CORS + 204 on OPTIONS? use existing test harness via a tiny inline check\ncat > /tmp/cors_smoke_test.go <<'EOF'\npackage api\nimport (\"net/http\";\"net/http/httptest\";\"testing\")\nfunc TestCORSSmoke(t *testing.T){\n  h := corsMiddleware(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request){ w.WriteHeader(200) }))\n  // OPTIONS short-circuits 204\n  r1 := httptest.NewRequest(\"OPTIONS\",\"/v0/pipes/x.json\",nil); w1 := httptest.NewRecorder(); h.ServeHTTP(w1,r1)\n  if w1.Code!=204 || w1.Header().Get(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\")!=\"*\" { t.Fatalf(\"OPTIONS: code=%d origin=%q\", w1.Code, w1.Header().Get(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\")) }\n  // GET passes through with header\n  r2 := httptest.NewRequest(\"GET\",\"/v0/pipes/x.json\",nil); w2 := httptest.NewRecorder(); h.ServeHTTP(w2,r2)\n  if w2.Code!=200 || w2.Header().Get(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\")!=\"*\" { t.Fatalf(\"GET: code=%d origin=%q\", w2.Code, w2.Header().Get(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\")) }\n}\nEOF\ncp /tmp/cors_smoke_test.go internal/api/zz_cors_smoke_test.go\ngo test ./internal/api/ -run TestCORSSmoke -count=1 2>&1 | tail -5\nrm internal/api/zz_cors_smoke_test.go",

  "content": "\"use client\";\n\nimport { useEffect, useState } from \"react\";\nimport { Bar } from \"@/components/charts/bar\";\nimport { BarChart } from \"@/components/charts/bar-chart\";\nimport { BarXAxis } from \"@/components/charts/bar-x-axis\";\nimport { BarYAxis } from \"@/components/charts/bar-y-axis\";\nimport { Grid } from \"@/components/charts/grid\";\n\n// Live prod endpoints + read-only demo token (scope READ:top_pages,\n// READ:views_over_time — 403 on anything else). Public by design.\nconst DEMO_API = \"https://tiny-api.ravencloak.org/v0/pipes\";\nconst DEMO_TOKEN = \"REDACTED\";\n\ntype Row = Record<string, unknown>;\ntype Status = \"loading\" | \"ready\" | \"error\";\n\nasync function fetchPipe(path: string): Promise<Row[]> {\n  const res = await fetch(`${DEMO_API}/${path}&token=${DEMO_TOKEN}`);\n  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`${path}: ${res.status}`);\n  const json = (await res.json()) as { data: Row[] };\n  return json.data ?? [];\n}\n\nexport function LiveDashboard() {\n  const [status, setStatus] = useState<Status>(\"loading\");\n  const [pages, setPages] = useState<{ path: string; views: number }[]>([]);\n  const [hourly, setHourly] = useState<{ hour: string; views: number }[]>([]);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    let alive = true;\n    (async () => {\n      try {\n        const [top, series] = await Promise.all([\n          fetchPipe(\"top_pages.json?limit=6\"),\n          fetchPipe(\"views_over_time.json?\"),\n        ]);\n        if (!alive) return;\n        setPages(\n          top.map((r) => ({ path: String(r.path), views: Number(r.views) })),\n        );\n        setHourly(\n          series.map((r) => ({\n            // \"2026-07-01 10:00:00\" -> \"10:00\"\n            hour: String(r.hour).slice(11, 16),\n            views: Number(r.views),\n          })),\n        );\n        setStatus(\"ready\");\n      } catch {\n        if (alive) setStatus(\"error\");\n      }\n    })();\n    return () => {\n      alive = false;\n    };\n  }, []);\n\n  if (status === \"error\") {\n    return (\n      <div className=\"rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/40 p-6 text-sm text-zinc-400\">\n        Live demo API unreachable right now. Raw endpoints:{\" \"}\n        <a\n          className=\"text-violet-300 hover:underline\"\n          href={`${DEMO_API}/top_pages.json?limit=5&token=${DEMO_TOKEN}`}\n          target=\"_blank\"\n          rel=\"noreferrer\"\n        >\n          top_pages.json\n        </a>\n      </div>\n    );\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"grid gap-6 sm:grid-cols-2\">\n      <ChartCard title=\"Top pages\" unit=\"views\">\n        <BarChart\n          data={status === \"loading\" ? [] : pages}\n          xDataKey=\"path\"\n          status={status === \"loading\" ? \"loading\" : \"ready\"}\n          aspectRatio=\"16 / 10\"\n          barGap={0.35}\n          margin={{ top: 20, right: 12, bottom: 40, left: 40 }}\n        >\n          <Grid horizontal numTicksRows={4} />\n          <Bar dataKey=\"views\" fill=\"#a78bfa\" lineCap={6} />\n          <BarXAxis />\n          <BarYAxis maxLabels={4} />\n        </BarChart>\n      </ChartCard>\n\n      <ChartCard title=\"Views over time\" unit=\"per hour\">\n        <BarChart\n          data={status === \"loading\" ? [] : hourly}\n          xDataKey=\"hour\"\n          status={status === \"loading\" ? \"loading\" : \"ready\"}\n          aspectRatio=\"16 / 10\"\n          barGap={0.25}\n          margin={{ top: 20, right: 12, bottom: 40, left: 40 }}\n        >\n          <Grid horizontal numTicksRows={4} />\n          <Bar dataKey=\"views\" fill=\"#818cf8\" lineCap={6} />\n          <BarXAxis />\n          <BarYAxis maxLabels={4} />\n        </BarChart>\n      </ChartCard>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction ChartCard({\n  title,\n  unit,\n  children,\n}: {\n  title: string;\n  unit: string;\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <div className=\"rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/40 p-5\">\n      <div className=\"flex items-baseline justify-between\">\n        <h4 className=\"text-sm font-medium text-zinc-100\">{title}</h4>\n        <span className=\"text-xs text-zinc-500\">{unit}</span>\n      </div>\n      <div className=\"mt-2\">{children}</div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"

[];\n}\n\nexport function LiveDashboard() {\n  const [status, setStatus] = useState<Status>(\"loading\");\n  const [pages, setPages] = useState<{ path: string; views: number }[]>([]);\n  const [hourly, setHourly] = useState<{ hour: string; views: number }[]>([]);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    let alive = true;\n    (async () => {\n      try {\n        const [top, series] = await Promise.all([\n          fetchPipe(\"top_pages.json?limit=6\"),\n          fetchPipe(\"views_over_time.json?\"),\n        ]);\n        if (!alive) return;\n        setPages(\n          top.map((r) => ({ path: String(r.path), views: Number(r.views) })),\n        );\n        setHourly(\n          series.map((r) => ({\n            hour: String(r.hour).slice(11, 16), // \"…10:00:00\" -> \"10:00\"\n            views: Number(r.views),\n          })),\n        );\n        setStatus(\"ready\");\n      } catch {\n        if (alive) setStatus(\"error\");\n      }\n    })();\n    return () => {\n      alive = false;\n    };\n  }, []);\n\n  if (status === \"error\") {\n    return (\n      <div className=\"rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/40 p-6 text-sm text-zinc-400\">\n        Live demo API unreachable right now — raw endpoint:{\" \"}\n        <a\n          className=\"text-violet-300 hover:underline\"\n          href={`${DEMO_API}/top_pages.json?limit=5&token=${DEMO_TOKEN}`}\n          target=\"_blank\"\n          rel=\"noreferrer\"\n        >\n          top_pages.json\n        </a>\n      </div>\n    );\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"grid gap-6 sm:grid-cols-2\">\n      <ChartCard title=\"Top pages\" unit=\"views\" loading={status === \"loading\"}>\n        <BarChart data={pages} margin={{ top: 8, right: 8, bottom: 4, left: -16 }}>\n          {gridAndAxes(\"path\")}\n          <Bar dataKey=\"views\" radius={[4, 4, 0, 0]} maxBarSize={40}>\n            {pages.map((_, i) => (\n              <Cell key={i} fill=\"#a78bfa\" />\n            ))}\n          </Bar>\n        </BarChart>\n      </ChartCard>\n\n      <ChartCard title=\"Views over time\" unit=\"per hour\" loading={status === \"loading\"}>\n        <BarChart data={hourly} margin={{ top: 8, right: 8, bottom: 4, left: -16 }}>\n          {gridAndAxes(\"hour\")}\n          <Bar dataKey=\"views\" radius={[4, 4, 0, 0]} maxBarSize={28} fill=\"#818cf8\" />\n        </BarChart>\n      </ChartCard>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nconst tick = { fill: \"#a1a1aa\", fontSize: 11 };\n\nfunction gridAndAxes(xKey: string) {\n  return (\n    <>\n      <CartesianGrid vertical={false} stroke=\"rgba(255,255,255,0.06)\" />\n      <XAxis\n        dataKey={xKey}\n        tick={tick}\n        tickLine={false}\n        axisLine={false}\n        interval={0}\n        angle={xKey === \"path\" ? -20 : 0}\n        textAnchor={xKey === \"path\" ? \"end\" : \"middle\"}\n        height={xKey === \"path\" ? 48 : 24}\n      />\n      <YAxis tick={tick} tickLine={false} axisLine={false} width={40} allowDecimals={false} />\n      <Tooltip\n        cursor={{ fill: \"rgba(255,255,255,0.04)\" }}\n        contentStyle={{\n          background: \"#09090b\",\n          border: \"1px solid );\n}\n\nfunction ChartCard({\n  title,\n  unit,\n  loading,\n  children,\n}: {\n  title: string;\n  unit: string;\n  loading: boolean;\n  children: React.ReactElement;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <div className=\"rounded-2xl border border-white/10 bg-zinc-950/40 p-5\">\n      <div className=\"flex items-baseline justify-between\">\n        <h4 className=\"text-sm font-medium text-zinc-100\">{title}</h4>\n        <span className=\"text-xs text-zinc-500\">{unit}</span>\n      </div>\n      <div className=\"mt-3 h-56\">\n        {loading ?
